From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n13\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.9\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, and welcome. I'm Dr. [Name], a psychiatrist. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, doctor, I'm here because my head just feels... cloudy lately. I manage a few properties, so I'm used to making decisions all day. But lately, I'll just sit there staring at a board packet or a vendor email, and I just can't seem to decide on even the simplest things. Things I used to handle fast, without a second thought. It's just really frustrating.\n\nDoctor: Over what period of time did you notice this change in your ability to make decisions? Was it sudden, over days, or has it been gradually worsening over weeks or months?\n\nPatient: Well, I'd say it's been going on for about two weeks now. It wasn't really a slow thing over months, it was a sharper change. It started right around the time my husband retired and was suddenly just home all day. First, there was this shift in my energy and my confidence, and then... well, then it just dropped into this low, cloudy feeling I have now. So, yeah, about two weeks.\n\nDoctor: Over these past two weeks, how has your sleep been? Are you sleeping more, less, or about the same as usual?\n\nPatient: Well, I haven't really noticed a big change with my sleep. I'd say I'm sleeping about the same amount as I usually do. It hasn't been a problem for me.\n\nDoctor: Besides the cloudiness and difficulty with decisions, have you been feeling sad, down, or depressed over these past two weeks?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't really say I feel sad or depressed. It's not like I'm sitting around crying or feeling down in the dumps. It's... well, it's really just that cloudy feeling I mentioned, and just being frustrated that I can't seem to think straight and do my job right now. But no, not sad.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any recent fever, illness, or infection? Also, have there been any changes to medications you take, or have you started anything new in the past few weeks?\n\nPatient: No, no fever or anything like that. I haven't been sick at all lately. And... no, I haven't changed any of my medications, either. I haven't started taking anything new. Physically, everything has been pretty much normal.\n\nDoctor: Besides the cloudiness and difficulty making decisions, have you noticed any other changes in how your mind or body is working? For example, have you had any trouble concentrating or remembering things, or any physical symptoms like headaches, dizziness, or body aches?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything else like that. My memory seems fine, and I haven't had any headaches or dizziness or anything physically wrong. It's really just that cloudy feeling and not being able to figure out what to do next at work. Everything else is pretty much the same as always.\n\nDoctor: When you think about things you normally enjoy doing - whether that's hobbies, time with friends, or activities outside of work - do you still find pleasure in those things, or does nothing seem as enjoyable to you right now?\n\nPatient: Well, no, I guess I haven't really been enjoying much. I just... I haven't wanted to go to my dance class, or go look at the little galleries like I normally do. I haven't even wanted to cook Sunday food for my family lately. But right now, I just don't have any interest in doing them.\n\nDoctor: When you say you're feeling cloudy and frustrated, do you also notice that your overall mood feels low or depressed, even if you're not actively crying or feeling sad in an obvious way?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd call it depressed, exactly. I don't feel sad, really, just sort of... empty, I guess. It's... it's not really a physical heaviness. My body feels fine. It's just that the blank, empty feeling is pretty much where my head is at. That's just how I feel on the inside right now.\n\nDoctor: When you think about your day-to-day life right now, would you say that most of the time you're feeling this empty, blank way, or does your mood shift around throughout the day?\n\nPatient: I'd say it's pretty much most of the time right now. It doesn't really shift around or change depending on the time of day or what I'm doing. It's just sort of... there. Constant, I guess you could say.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about death or dying, or any thoughts of harming yourself?\n\nPatient: Well, there have been a few mornings lately where I've had this scary thought... just thinking that maybe it would be easier if I didn't wake up. But I don't want to hurt myself, and I don't have any kind of plan to do anything like that. It's just a thought that crosses my mind sometimes.\n\nDoctor: When you have that thought about it being easier if you didn't wake up, how often does that happen - is it just occasionally in the morning, or does it come up multiple times throughout the day?\n\nPatient: It's really just occasionally. I'd say maybe... two or three times a week, usually right when I wake up. It doesn't really pop into my head during the day when I'm busy doing things. Just in those quiet moments in the morning.\n\nDoctor: During these past two weeks, have you been feeling guilty about things, or like you're worthless or to blame for problems - or has that not been something you've experienced?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, I have been feeling guilty lately. I manage a community property, and... I just keep thinking I let the residents down. I spoke a bit too big, I guess, promising them things before I actually checked the money to see if we could do it. Nobody has actually come out and said I ruined anything, but I just can't shake the feeling that I messed up.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. It's... it's not really a physical heaviness. My body feels fine. It's just that the blank, empty feeling is pretty much where my head is at. That's just how I feel on the inside right now.\n\nDoctor: When you think about your day-to-day life right now, would you say that most of the time you're feeling this empty, blank way, or does your mood shift around throughout the day?\n\nPatient: I'd say it's pretty much most of the time right now. Constant, I guess you could say.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about death or dying, or any thoughts of harming yourself?\n\nPatient: Well, there have been a few mornings lately where I've had this scary thought... just thinking that maybe it would be easier if I didn't wake up. But I don't want to hurt myself, and I don't have any kind of plan to do anything like that. It's just a thought that crosses my mind sometimes.\n\nDoctor: When you have that thought about it being easier if you didn't wake up, how often does that happen - is it just occasionally in the morning, or does it come up multiple times throughout the day?\n\nPatient: It's really just occasionally. I manage a community property, and... I just keep thinking I let the residents down. Nobody has actually come out and said I ruined anything, but I just can't shake the feeling that I messed up.\n\nDoctor: Just to be clear about the past two weeks - would you say that this empty, blank feeling and the loss of interest in things you normally enjoy have been present on most days, or have there been several days where you felt more like your usual self?\n\nPatient: Well... And... no, I haven't changed any of my medications, either. I haven't started taking anything new. Physically, everything has been pretty much normal.\n\nDoctor: Besides the cloudiness and difficulty making decisions, have you noticed any other changes in how your mind or body is working? For example, have you had any trouble concentrating or remembering things, or any physical symptoms like headaches, dizziness, or body aches?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything else like that. My memory seems fine, and I haven't had any headaches or dizziness or anything physically wrong. It's really just that cloudy feeling and not being able to figure out what to do next at work. Everything else is pretty much the same as always.\n\nDoctor: When you think about things you normally enjoy doing - whether that's hobbies, time with friends, or activities outside of work - do you still find pleasure in those things, or does nothing seem as enjoyable to you right now?\n\nPatient: Well, no, I guess I haven't really been enjoying much. I just... I haven't wanted to go to my dance class, or go look at the little galleries like I normally do. I haven't even wanted to cook Sunday food for my family lately. And those are the things that usually make me feel like myself, you know? But right now, I just don't have any interest in doing them.\n\nDoctor: When you say you're feeling cloudy and frustrated, do you also notice that your overall mood feels low or depressed, even if you're not actively crying or feeling sad in an obvious way?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd call it depressed, exactly. I'm not... I'm not sitting around crying or feeling miserable like that. It's more just this heavy, blank feeling. I'm just tired and frustrated that I can't think straight. I don't feel sad, really, just sort of... empty, I guess. Like I'm just going through the motions.\n\nDoctor: When you describe that heavy, blank, empty feeling you're having - would you say that's the main way your mood feels right now, or is your mood actually okay and it's more just a physical sensation of heaviness?\n\nPatient: Well, I wouldn't say my mood is okay, no. It's... it's not really a physical heaviness. My body feels fine. It's just that the blank, empty feeling is pretty much where my head is at. That's just how I feel on the inside right now.\n\nDoctor: When you think about your day-to-day life right now, would you say that most of the time you're feeling this empty, blank way, or does your mood shift around throughout the day?\n\nPatient: I'd say it's pretty much most of the time right now. It doesn't really shift around or change depending on the time of day or what I'm doing. It's just sort of... there. Constant, I guess you could say.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about death or dying, or any thoughts of harming yourself?\n\nPatient: Well, there have been a few mornings lately where I've had this scary thought... just thinking that maybe it would be easier if I didn't wake up. But I don't want to hurt myself, and I don't have any kind of plan to do anything like that. It's just a thought that crosses my mind sometimes.\n\nDoctor: When you have that thought about it being easier if you didn't wake up, how often does that happen - is it just occasionally in the morning, or does it come up multiple times throughout the day?\n\nPatient: It's really just occasionally. I'd say maybe... two or three times a week, usually right when I wake up. It doesn't really pop into my head during the day when I'm busy doing things. Just in those quiet moments in the morning.\n\nDoctor: During these past two weeks, have you been feeling guilty about things, or like you're worthless or to blame for problems - or has that not been something you've experienced?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, I have been feeling guilty lately. I manage a community property, and... I just keep thinking I let the residents down. I spoke a bit too big, I guess, promising them things before I actually checked the money to see if we could do it. Nobody has actually come out and said I ruined anything, but I just can't shake the feeling that I messed up.\n\nDoctor: Just to be clear about the past two weeks - would you say that this empty, blank feeling and the loss of interest in things you normally enjoy have been present on most days, or have there been several days where you felt more like your usual self?\n\nPatient: Well... I'm not really sure, to be honest. I haven't kept track of the days like that. I guess I haven't really noticed if it's been most days, or if there were a few normal ones mixed in. I just know I've been feeling that way lately, but I couldn't tell you exactly how often it was.\n\nDoctor: Let me ask this another way - over these past two weeks, on how many days would you say you've been feeling that empty, blank way? Was it almost every day, or were there several days where you felt more normal?\n\nPatient: I honestly still couldn't give you a number. It... it just sort of all runs together lately. It feels like it's been a lot of the time, but I really couldn't swear to whether it was almost every day or if there were a couple of normal ones mixed in. I'm sorry, I just really don't know.\n\nDoctor: I know it's hard to remember exactly, but thinking back over these two weeks - would you say that on most days you've been having that empty, blank feeling, or would you say it's been less than half the days?\n\nPatient: I really just don't know. Like I said, it all just kind of blends together right now. I couldn't tell you if it was most days or less than half. I just know it's been happening, but I haven't been paying attention to exactly which days or how many.\n\nDoctor: Let me try asking it differently. If I were to ask your husband or a family member how many days in the past two weeks you've seemed like yourself versus how many days you've had that empty, blank feeling - what do you think they would say?\n\nPatient: I don't think my husband would be able to tell you either, to be honest. He... he just goes about his day, and I go about mine. He might say I've been a bit quiet lately, but I really doubt he's keeping track of the good days versus the blank days any more than I am. So... I really couldn't guess what number he'd give you.\n\nDoctor: I understand it's hard to remember exactly. But let me ask you this - thinking about yesterday and today, and the few days before that - have you been feeling that empty, blank way on these recent days, or have you been feeling more like yourself?\n\nPatient: Well, thinking about yesterday and today... yes, I've had that low, cloudy feeling. Honestly, this whole shift... the sharper change in my energy and my confidence, and then dropping into this blank feeling... it's all been going on for about two weeks now. It really started right when my husband retired and was suddenly just... home all day.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
